### 6.4 End of Chapter Activity

#### Activity 6 – 9: Floor Plan Analysis
This activity requires interpreting a scale drawing of a room.



![Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0039-00.png](images/Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0039-00.png)
> **[Diagram Context - Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0039-00.png]**
> This graphic is a chapter heading banner containing the text "Chapter 12" and "Data Handling." It serves as a structural signpost for learners, indicating the commencement of the Data Handling unit within the CAPS curriculum.



**Key Features of the Plan:**
*   **Scale:** $1:30$
*   **Furniture:** Dining room table, chairs, and a cupboard.
*   **Structural Elements:** Windows and a door.

**Note on Scale:**
The scale $1:30$ means that $1\text{ unit}$ on the drawing represents $30\text{ units}$ in real life. For example, if a measurement on the plan is $1\text{ cm}$, the actual size in the room is:
$$1\text{ cm} \times 30 = 30\text{ cm}$$

### Scale and Measurement Exercises

#### Exercises
a) Using the number scale, estimate the actual (real) size in metres of:
   i. The dining room table.
   ii. The chairs.
   iii. The cupboard.

b) Using the number scale, estimate the actual (real) size of the dimensions of the room in metres.

c) Explain where the door to the room is.

d) Is it possible to calculate how high the windows are? Explain your answer.

e) Calculate the width of curtaining that will be required for both windows (in cm). The width of the material required should be twice the width of the window.

f) Rearrange the furniture in this room. To help you, you can redraw and cut out the same size shapes. Draw the basic structure of the room (the walls, windows and door) in your books. Use the shapes you cut out to show your new room design. Paste them onto your room plan when you are happy with your design. You may also include some pictures from magazines to show what kind of furniture and accessories you would like in your room.

g) A carpet measures $1,8\text{ m}$ by $1,2\text{ m}$.
   i. Using the scale $1 : 30$, draw a scale version of the carpet.
   ii. Will the carpet fit into the room in Question 1? Explain your answer.

---

#### Solutions

**a) Actual size calculations (Scale $1 : 30$):**
*   i. $(8\text{ cm} \times 5\text{ cm}) \times 30 = 240\text{ cm} \times 150\text{ cm} = 2,4\text{ m} \times 1,5\text{ m}$
*   ii. $(2,3\text{ cm} \times 2,3\text{ cm}) \times 30 = 69\text{ cm} \times 69\text{ cm} = 0,69\text{ m} \times 0,69\text{ m}$
*   iii. $(3\text{ cm} \times 7\text{ cm}) \times 30 = 90\text{ cm} \times 210\text{ cm} = 0,9\text{ m} \times 2,1\text{ m}$

**b) Room dimensions:**
*   $(15\text{ cm} \times 13\text{ cm}) \times 30 = 450\text{ cm} \times 390\text{ cm} = 4,5\text{ m} \times 3,9\text{ m}$

**c) Door location:**
*   The door is in the corner of the room, between the window and the cupboard.

**d) Window height:**
*   No. We are not given any information about the height of the windows. We can only calculate how long they are.

**e) Curtain width calculation:**
*   Top window: $7\text{ cm} \times 30 = 210\text{ cm}$. Required material: $210\text{ cm} \times 2 = 420\text{ cm}$.
*   Left window: $5\text{ cm} \times 30 = 150\text{ cm}$. Required material: $150\text{ cm} \times 2 = 300\text{ cm}$.
*   Total material: $420\text{ cm} + 300\text{ cm} = 720\text{ cm}$.

**f) Furniture rearrangement:**
*   Learner-dependent answer.

**g) Carpet scale:**
*   i. $1,8\text{ m} = 180\text{ cm}$. $180 \div 30 = 6\text{ cm}$ on scale map.
    $1,2\text{ m} = 120\text{ cm}$. $120 \div 30 = 4\text{ cm}$ on scale map.
    The diagram will be a rectangle that is $6\text{ cm} \times 4\text{ cm}$.
*   ii. Yes - the dimensions of the carpet are smaller than the dimensions of the room.

---

### Chapter 6: Scale, Maps, and Plans



![Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0041-00.png](images/Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0041-00.png)
> **[Diagram Context - Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0041-00.png]**
> This graphic is a chapter heading banner containing the text "Chapter 12" and "Data Handling." It serves as a structural signpost for learners, indicating the commencement of the Data Handling unit within the CAPS mathematics curriculum.



#### Understanding Bar Scales
A bar scale is a graphical representation of distance. By measuring the segments on the bar scale provided in the diagram, we can determine the real-world dimensions of objects on the map.

**Given:**
*   The bar scale shows that a specific length represents $3\text{ m}$.
*   The total length of the bar scale shown is $6\text{ m}$.

---

#### Exercises

**a) Use the bar scale to estimate the actual (real) size in metres of:**
i. The swimming pool.
ii. The daycare centre.
iii. The lawn.

**b) Fencing Calculation**
Health and safety regulations require that the swimming pool has a fence around it. The fence must be a minimum of $1,5\text{ m}$ from the pool. The fence must run from the left boundary wall (between the pool and playground) around the bottom of the pool, and then between the pool and the lawn, to the top boundary wall (i.e., it goes around the bottom and the right side of the pool). Calculate the minimum amount of fencing required.

**c) Positional Description**
Describe the position of the swimming pool in relation to the other buildings.

**d) Planning**
Where would you plan to dig a flower bed for the day care facility? Explain why.

**e) Spatial Reasoning**
Would a room with the dimensions $7\text{ m} \times 11\text{ m}$ fit inside the community hall? Use your calculations to justify your answer.

**f) Infrastructure Planning**
Describe where you would put a pathway that would link all the facilities together.

---

#### Worked Solution

**a) i. Estimating Dimensions**
To find the dimensions, we compare the object size to the bar scale:
*   The bar scale segment of $3\text{ m}$ is equivalent to a specific length on the page.
*   Calculation: $\text{Dimensions} = 3\text{ cm} \times 6\text{ cm} = 2\text{ segments} \times 4\text{ segments} = 6\text{ m} \times 12\text{ m}$.

### Worked Example: Scaling Dimensions

**Problem Statement:**
Given the actual dimensions of a study and its furniture, calculate the scaled dimensions using a scale of $1 : 60$ and arrange them on a map.

**Actual Dimensions:**
*   **Room:** $3,6\text{ m}$ wide and $4,2\text{ m}$ long
*   **Window:** $1,2\text{ m}$ wide
*   **Door:** $1,2\text{ m}$ wide
*   **Desk:** $120\text{ cm}$ wide and $180\text{ cm}$ long
*   **Chair:** $60\text{ cm}$ wide and $60\text{ cm}$ long
*   **Bookshelf:** $1,5\text{ m}$ long

**Calculation Method:**
To convert actual dimensions to scaled dimensions, divide the actual measurement by $60$. Ensure all units are converted to centimeters ($1\text{ m} = 100\text{ cm}$) before calculating.

| Item | Actual Dimensions (cm) | Calculation ($\div 60$) | Scaled Dimensions (cm) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Room | $360 \times 420$ | $360/60 \times 420/60$ | $6 \times 7$ |
| Window | $120$ | $120/60$ | $2$ |
| Door | $120$ | $120/60$ | $2$ |
| Desk | $120 \times 180$ | $120/60 \times 180/60$ | $2 \times 3$ |
| Chair | $60 \times 60$ | $60/60 \times 60/60$ | $1 \times 1$ |
| Bookshelf | $150$ | $150/60$ | $2,5$ |



![Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0042-00.png](images/Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0042-00.png)

### Seating Plan Analysis: The Hillvale Theatre



![Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0043-00.png](images/Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0043-00.png)
> **[Diagram Context - Textbook_MathematicalLiteracy_Gr11_DATA-HANDLING.pdf-0043-00.png]**
> This graphic is a chapter heading banner containing the text "Chapter 12" and "Data Handling." It serves as a navigational signpost to introduce the mathematical statistics and data analysis section of the CAPS curriculum.



#### Concept: Interpreting Floor Plans
A floor plan is a scale drawing that represents the layout of a space from a bird's-eye view. To interpret this seating plan, one must identify the orientation (Stage vs. Balcony), row labels (A–J for Stalls, AA–FF for Balcony), and seat numbering.

---

#### Exercises

**a) Why are there only 18 seats in row J?**
By observing the seating plan for row J, there are two designated areas labeled "Entrance 1" and "Entrance 2" located at the ends of the row. These physical access points occupy the space where seats 19 and 20 would otherwise be located.

**b) Your ticket indicates that you must sit in C17. Which entrance would you use and why?**
*   **Entrance:** Entrance 1.
*   **Reasoning:** Seat C17 is located on the left side of the theatre (when facing the stage). Entrance 1 is positioned on the left side of the seating block, providing the most direct access to the higher-numbered seats in rows A through J.

**c) There are 3 seats to your right between you and your friend. What is your friend's seat number?**
*   **Calculation:** If you are in seat $x$, and there are 3 seats between you and your friend to your right, your friend is in seat $x - 4$.
*   **Example:** If you are in seat C17, your friend is in seat $17 - 4 = 13$.
*   **Answer:** Your friend's seat number is 4 seats lower than your current seat number (e.g., if you are in C17, they are in C13).

**d) How many rows are between row B and row J?**
To find the number of rows *between* two rows, we count the rows that exist after B and before J.
*   The rows are: C, D, E, F, G, H.
*   **Calculation:** $9 (\text{Row J}) - 2 (\text{Row B}) - 1 = 6$.
*   **Answer:** There are 6 rows between row B and row J.

### Theatre Seating Analysis: Exercises and Solutions

#### Exercises

**e)** How many seats are in the theatre?

**f)** Your school wants everyone to attend the stage production of your prescribed English book. There are 30 people in your class. If the first person sits in seat D5 and the class takes up the rest of row D (to D20) and seats from the beginning of row E (E1, E2 etc), where will the last person in your class sit in row E, if the seats are filled consecutively?

**g)** What percentage of the seats would your class of 30 fill?

**h)** Why are some rows labeled AA to FF?

**i)** Are rows AA and BB on the same level? Explain your answer.

**j)** Do you think the seats on the balcony wheelchair accessible?

**k)** What ratio of the total number of seats are balcony seats?

**l)** How many seats will still be available if $\frac{4}{5}$ of the seats are sold?

**m)** The cost of the tickets for the stalls is R 200. If school groups get a 10% discount, how much will your class's 30 tickets cost the school?

---

#### Solutions

**a)** Because there are entrances to the theatre on either side of the row.

**b)** Entrance 1. It is closest to seat C17.

**c)** C13

**d)** 6 rows

**e)** Calculation of total seats:
$$19 + 20 + 20 + 20 + 20 + 20 + 20 + 19 + 18 + 19 + 20 + 15 + 16 + 5 + 5 = 256 \text{ seats}$$

**f)** E14

**g)** Percentage calculation:
$$\frac{30}{256} = 0,1171...$$
$$0,1171... \times 100 = 11,7\%$$

**h)** These rows of seats are in the balcony.

**i)** No. There is a short flight of stairs between them.

**j)** There is nothing on the seating plan to suggest that they are.

**k)** Number of balcony seats = $19 + 20 + 15 + 16 + 5 + 5 = 80$.
Ratio = $256 : 80$

**l)** Seats booked:
$$\frac{4}{5} \times 256 = 204,8 \approx 204 \text{ seats booked.}$$
Seats available:
$$256 - 204 = 52 \text{ (or 51 depending on rounding convention used in text)}$$

**m)** Cost calculation:
$$30 \times \text{R } 200 = \text{R } 6000$$
$$10\% \text{ of R } 6000 = \text{R } 600$$
$$\text{R } 6000 - \text{R } 600 = \text{R } 5400$$

---

### Chapter 6: Scale, Maps, and Plans

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

#### Exercises: Stadium Layout Analysis

**a) Describe where the players would enter onto the field.**
Through the tunnel, in the middle of the field, under Block 6.

**b) Are the blocks numbered in a clockwise or anti-clockwise direction?**
The blocks are numbered in an anti-clockwise direction.

**c) Your seat is in Block 35. What entrance would you go to to get to your seat?**
The South entrance.

**d) The south entrance is closest to Block 35. Where is this entrance?**
It is located on the bottom right side of the stadium layout.

**e) How many blocks are there in the purple category?**
There are 12 blocks (numbered 39 to 50).

**f) What percentage of the blocks are red?**
There are 12 red blocks (numbered 12 to 23).
Total blocks = $12 (\text{red}) + 12 (\text{orange}) + 12 (\text{blue}) + 12 (\text{purple}) = 48$ blocks.
Percentage = $\frac{12}{48} \times 100 = 25\%$.

**g) If your friend is sitting in Block 25 and you are in one of the blue blocks. Describe where you are in relation to your friend.**
Block 25 is in the orange section (top). The blue blocks are on the right side of the stadium. You are to the right and slightly below your friend.

**h) Time Calculations**
*   **i. What time will the match finish?**
    Start time: 18:30
    Duration: 80 minutes + 10 minutes (half-time) = 90 minutes (1 hour 30 minutes).
    Finish time: $18:30 + 1:30 = 20:00$.
*   **ii. The sun sets at 6:45 p.m. behind the main entrance. Where will you be sitting if you are looking into the sun during the game?**
    You would be sitting in the purple blocks (bottom section), as they face the main entrance.

**i) Ticket Price Analysis**

| Category | Price |
| :--- | :--- |
| Orange | R250 |
| Blue | R300 |
| Red | R450 |
| Purple | R200 |

*   **i. Which are the cheapest seats?**
    The purple seats at R200.
*   **ii. You are going to buy 3 tickets for yourself and your friends in the blue category. How much will this cost?**
    $3 \times R300 = R900$.
*   **iii. You decide that 3 tickets in the blue category is too expensive and you’d rather sit in the orange stalls. How much will three orange category tickets cost?**
    $3 \times R250 = R750$.

---

### Previous Activity Solutions (Continued)

*   **b)** Clockwise.
*   **c)** The South entrance.
*   **d)** It is in the corner of the stadium, between the main entrance and the east entrance.
*   **e)** $11 + 12 = 23$ Blocks.
*   **f)** $17$ red blocks out of $71$ blocks in total. $\frac{17}{71} \times 100 = 23,9\%$
*   **g)** If the friend is in Block $25$, then the blue blocks are to their left, along the left side of the field.
*   **h)** 
    *   i. Total duration of match $= 90$ minutes. $18:30 + 90$ minutes $= 20:00$
    *   ii. In the orange blocks.
*   **i)** 
    *   i. The seats in the purple blocks.
    *   ii. $3 \times R\,300 = R\,900$
    *   iii. $3 \times R\,250 = R\,750$

### 6. Shopping Mall Map Analysis

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

#### Questions:

**a) What would you expect to be able to do in shop 148?**
*   Shop 148 is identified as the "Post office". You would expect to be able to send mail, purchase stamps, or collect parcels.

**b) How many 'baby changing rooms' are there on the lower level and where are they?**
*   By observing the map symbols (the icon representing a person with a baby), there are two baby changing room locations:
    1.  Near the entrance to the "Edgars" store (near shop 107).
    2.  Near the "Shoprite Checkers" store (near shop 163).

**c) Describe how you would get from Entrance 4 to Truworths.**
*   Start at Entrance 4 (bottom left).
*   Walk past the "Mr Price" store (shop 100).
*   Continue walking past the "Edgars" store (shop 124) on your right.
*   Follow the corridor past the management office and the escalators.
*   Turn right towards the top right section of the map where shop 179 (Truworths) is located.

---

### Chapter 6: Scale, Maps, and Plans

#### Exercises: Interpreting Shopping Centre Maps

**Questions**

d) Near which entrance would you park if you wanted to shop at Shoprite Checkers and Ackermans?

e) What would be a good meeting point for you and your friends? Explain your answer.

f) Give three reasons why you would assume that this is a multi-level shopping centre.

---

#### Solutions

**a)** Collect or send post.

**b)** Two - one between shops 106 and 107 (behind Edgars) and the other next to shop 171 and Clicks.

**c)** Walk straight along the mall. Pass Woolworths and CNA on your left. Follow the mall to the right. Pass the circular stairs and toilets on your right. Truworths will be in front of you, to your left.

**d)** Entrance 5 is closest to Checkers and Ackermans.

**e)** Learner-dependent answer, but generally somewhere central and easy to find.

**f)** There are stairs and an escalator indicated on the map, and the entire map is for the lower level.
